| J-R34 | Chose a per-viewer `stylePreset` view key over treating the canonical style as a last-writer-wins view key, because a viewer's look must neither rewrite the shared style nor silently diverge from it (§11.3; N-15). |
| J-R35 | Chose first-map budgets as laws of the building count, with the one-second knee and the miss above it declared, over one bar per rung, because size follows population inside a rung (§12.5; N-2). |
| J-R36 | Chose to state load verification as a law of the log (V = M(B) + Σ c(e) + S14 + 5 ms) over saving checkpoints, because checkpoints would multiply edit-heavy documents while the wait is already off the path to the picture (§12.6; N-16). |
| J-R37 | Chose to let a CI font test pick each face's small-caps and figure path — the face's features, the family's OFL SC cut, then synthesis — over assuming the GSUB features, because nothing in the project shows the default face carries them (§9.2; N-12). |
| J-R38 | Chose to read O3 (d)'s model-time bars over every size a rung builds and to declare where they hold — p95 up to ≈ 4 500 / 17 700 / 58 800 people, the max (each generation ≤ 1.25 × its own law) up to ≈ 3 400 / 13 600 / 45 800 — and the miss above them for the owner's veto, over reading them at the targets only, because size follows population inside a rung, as N-2 ruled for the first map, and a reading at the targets hides the miss; and chose to judge the max on outliers confirmed by the median of three solo re-timings over the maximum of the contended nightly screen, because one scheduling stall on a shared runner would otherwise fail the gate and, through the fallback, loosen the promise on noise (§12.3; NR-1, NR3-4). |
| J-R39 | Chose explicit passes on `OrderSlot` with most-specific-wins resolution and refusal of any unresolved or tied (semantic, pass), with `derived` entries for the raised under-copy and projected shadows, over one slot per semantic or an order fixed in code, because a way's casing and body must sit in different slots while sheets stay data (§8.2; NR-3). |
| J-R40 | Chose furniture-placement and snap queries answered by the tile worker within one frame — the first class of its queue, served between the steps of resumable raster jobs (`TileBackend.begin` → `RasterJob.step(8)`) — over copying mark kinds and vertices into the main thread's hit-test slices or placing furniture in the generation worker, because the slices would carry model-sized geometry and the generation worker's index has no kinds, vertices or placed labels; and chose the resumable job over a synchronous raster call because a worker serves its message queue only between calls (§8.9; NR-4, NR3-2). |
| J-R41 | Chose a transient display list built in the generation worker for SVG exports and released after writing, over handing SVG to the tile worker or keeping a second resident list, because the SVG writer reads the document, which the tile worker never holds, and exports are occasional (§11.1, §12.8; NR-5). |
| J-R42 | Chose a constant stream key for the stamp-tile library — (class, reference spacing, creation index: 0–3 for the four tiles, 4 for their shared border band), `STAGE_VIEW`, `render.stampTiles` — with integer-centimetre points, `dmath` transcendentals, index-order sums and ties and the stated removal update, built when the product is built by `plat/tools/stamptiles/` and shipped as a hash-checked asset, over a seed- or document-keyed library and over building it in each session, because the tiles are shared by every document, session and host, the per-town variety comes from the stamp's offset, rotation, spacing and seed-dependent variant, and a session build ran unbudgeted on the first district-band settle in both workers (§8.10; NR-6, NR3-7, NR4-1). |
| J-R43 | Chose a declared PLAT CityJSON Extension for `+platGeoAnchor`, validated in CI against the repository's schema, over a sidecar as the primary, with a `<name>.geoanchor.json` sidecar as the pre-decided fallback, because the extension keeps the anchor inside the one file a GIS user moves around and the sidecar leaves the CityJSON file valid by construction (§11.1; NR-7). |
| J-R44 | Chose to keep the derivation pool primary at each size where it runs within its law + 10 %, measures faster than a single worker on the same generations and gives identical digests for pool sizes 0–3 — each size range otherwise running the configuration faster at the nearest measured size — over one bar of p95 ≤ 300 ms at the CITY target, because the pooled law itself puts the default CITY request at 265–340 ms, so that bar would have replaced the pool by a single worker ≈ 85–115 ms slower exactly when the pool met its budget, and pool size never changes the model, so choosing per size range is configuration only (§12.3; NR3-1). |
| J-R45 | Chose a pin pass at placement time — a stop's pins in `moveLabel` order, a pin meeting an earlier fixed pin hidden at that stop and reported — with `moveLabel`'s refusal kept as the interactive guard, over refusing the warps, renames, rerolls and style changes that bring pins together, because those edits are not about labels and must not be blocked by one, while hiding a pin is a reported view change; and chose `moveLabel` order over priority order because the guard already gives the pin that stood first precedence and a stored `seq` does not move when priorities or areas change (§9.1, §9.2; NR3-3). |
| J-R46 | Chose four fixed classes in the tile worker's queue — queries, incoming deltas and band answers, the visible view, exports — run one resumable step at a time, with an export that has waited 100 ms given one step, over first-come order and over strict priority alone, because an A2 print's bands would otherwise hold newly exposed tiles behind up to ≈ 20 s of export work and strict priority lets an animated view stop an export; and chose to apply deltas ahead of the view's rasters because every tile that waits for one is visible work (§8.9; NR3-6). |
| J-R47 | Chose a transient display list, built by the generation worker and transferred with the request, for PNG, print and `.dd2vtt` exports, over rastering them from the view's list, because an edit made during a long export would change the view's list between two bands and tear the picture, and the view's list holds finer bands only for the districts the view has visited; the price is the export-time peak §12.8 already counts for SVG, now held in the tile worker, and its canvases counted against the tier's ceiling (§11.1, §12.8; NR3-6). |
| J-R48 | Chose one set of four stamp tiles per class at a reference spacing of 4 m, scaled per entity by its spacing ÷ 4 m, over a set per (class, spacing), because the spacing is drawn per entity in whole centimetres, so a per-spacing library has hundreds of members per class (≈ 19 ms each to build, megabytes as an asset), while uniformly scaled blue noise keeps its property, its ranks and its ordinals at one multiplication per point (§8.10; NR3-7). |
| J-R49 | Chose a timing phase — every gating CI timing taken last in its tier and alone on the runner, in calibrated batches — over a noise rule per check, because runner contention inflates every gating timing the same way (a five-seed p95 is in effect a maximum), so one mechanism protects the sweeps, the first-map runs, the pool comparison and the max-clause re-timings at ≤ 20 min of the nightly budget (§12.1; NR3-4). |
| J-R50 | Chose four seam-compatible stamp tiles per class sharing one border band — the points within half the reference spacing of a tile edge, cut from a fifth, full toroidal tile, each tile filling the rest by Bridson seeded with the band — over the independent variants (3.5–5 pairs of trees per 64-m seam closer than the spacing), over one larger toroidal tile (every instance position repeats on a periodic lattice), over a band of the full spacing (≈ 23 % of each cell's points repeated instead of ≈ 12 %) and over a band sampled by Bridson inside itself (a density stripe along every lattice line), because the half-width cut band gives 0 close pairs across every seam for every band's subset (measured on all 256 2 × 2 arrangements) while keeping ≈ 88 % of each cell varying and a full tile's density; the price is slightly larger holes along the band's inner edge (largest empty circle 1.20 × r₀ against ≤ 1.08) (§8.10; NR4-1). |
| J-R51 | Chose S13b as a resumable placement job — one decision per unit, the local evaluation on an explicit stack with the memo kept between steps — stepped in the tile worker's queue (class 3 for the view, class 4, first, for an export) over running it whole at a stop, because a large print's placement grows with the candidates in its frame and would hold queries and the view behind one unbounded unit, while every unit boundary is a safe stop that leaves the placement digest unchanged (§8.9, §9.2, §11.1, §12.7; NR4-2). |
| J-R52 | Chose integer-centimetre instance positions computed with `dmath` (R_θ from the integer `rotationCdeg`), the whole instance function under §6.5's allowlist lint, over double positions from the engine's `Math.cos`, because the model-format exporters write those positions and promise files byte-identical on every host, and the centimetre is the model's own lattice, so the face test is exact too; the price is ≤ 1.5 cm off the spacing (§8.10, §11.1; NR4-3). |
| J-R53 | Chose, for a PNG time miss, the None row filter on every row on every host — then zlib level 3 in Node, and in browsers, whose `CompressionStream` takes no level, the D-18 re-declaration — over zlib level 3 alone, which browsers cannot apply and which measured the smaller lever, and over a fixed Sub filter, which measured slower and larger than None on flat, relief and textured synthetic maps; and kept the per-row minimum-sum rule as the default because it gives the smallest file wherever rows carry gradients or texture (§8.8, §12.7; NR4-4). |
| J-R54 | Chose a light step — the path sun quantised to 15° of azimuth and 5° of altitude — for `faceLight`, the two-tone roofs, `hillshade` and the `lit` crown bake, keying the `under` and `over` layers (or `base`) by it and re-rastering them in a light pass that runs to completion, over lighting them by the 1° sun (a full re-raster per 1° step) and over keying only the shadows by the sun (roofs and crowns of several suns side by side, differing by tier); the fallback holds the fixed sun for all but the shadows (§8.4, §8.8, §8.10, §12.7; NR4-5). |
| J-R55 | Chose to build the tiles at build time and ship them over building them in each session because the session build ran on the interaction path — the first zoom to the district band, under §12.5's ≤ 150 ms settle — with no budget, in both workers, and NR-6 had already made the tiles a constant, so a build-time asset costs a bundled ≈ 31 KB and a hash check instead (NR3-7). (§8.10, archived l. 7874–7877) *(New row added by the consistency pass, 2026-09-26: the canon gave this inline passage, which had no row, the next free id.)* |
| J-R56 | Chose a max clause relative to each generation's own law over one absolute maximum per rung because a generation's building count varies with its seed, so only a ratio to its own law separates a slow generation from a large one. Chose confirmation by the median of three solo re-timings over the maximum of the contended screen because one garbage-collection pause or scheduling stall of ≈ 60 ms on a ≈ 240-ms CITY generation — exactly the tail a maximum over 12 000 contended timings picks up — would otherwise fail the gate with no change to the generator, and its fallback would then loosen the owner-facing promise on noise; a generation that is really slow is slow three times out of three (NR3-4). (§12.3, archived l. 8885–8891) *(New row added by the consistency pass, 2026-09-26: the canon gave this inline passage, which had no row, the next free id.)* |
| J-S1 | Chose to tag every acceptance letter C / P / O and to require a P or O letter for each first-weighted improvement over citing the existing numbers as evidence of improvement, because most of them hold by construction and would pass while the town reads worse than MFCG or FTG (IMP-4; §15.2). |
| J-S2 | Chose a written, blind side-by-side protocol with the owner as the declared rater over tester counts and an unstructured "owner's eye", because the program has one human and an eye without fixed seeds, blinding and a pass rule can be steered (IMP-4, IMP-11; §13.6). |
| J-S3 | Chose to give every threshold a number now, with a confirming milestone and a pre-decided fallback, over placeholders set by the corpus phase, because the corpus comes after the whole standalone program and placeholders either block a milestone or get promoted silently (IMP-6; §13.7). |
| J-S4 | Chose byte identity for Regenerate (keep settings) and, for New random, a calibrated rank test — each regenerated statistic's rank among K = 50 from-scratch generations of the same scope, ties broken by a uniform draw, a 10-bin χ² test per statistic with Holm at family-wise 0.01 over a nightly campaign of 800 scopes (400 block, 400 district; VS2-9) — over two-sample KS tests per regeneration and over a fixed 95 % pass rate of an empirical central band, because both of those fail a perfect regenerator (the band passes it only 70–79 % of the time on four statistics, executed) while ranks are exactly uniform for any K; the executed calibration — false-failure rates, detection at ρ = 0.8 and ρ = 1, and the 92–95 % that 400 scopes caught — is §13.2's; the randomised tie-break is what keeps a discrete statistic calibrated (without it a perfect regenerator failed every campaign) (IMP-7; §13.2, §15.2 O9 c). *(Amended by the consistency pass, 2026-09-26: the detection figures point to §13.2's executed counts instead of restating a bound (SCOPE-C03).)* |

| J-A28.21 | Chose to declare the plan rung's records in §2.6 with rooms as values on the world lattice — integer-centimetre rings read back from the kernel's transient arena (J-A28.31), a wing-local module as a table row (default 50 cm) that is only the planner's grid, room areas in m² by band, and the reference's cell surviving only as the VTT export's grid — and to keep `Composition.lod` at four values with `rooms` read as the optional principal rooms a Fantasy-Manor-class composer emits, which the plan takes as fixed rings, over a modelling cell, rooms as L4 plat faces, or folding `rooms` into `plan`, because the design's units are centimetres everywhere (§2.1) and a cell would make room sizes a second unit; plat faces would put every clicked plan into the arena and the model digest; and a composer's hall or tower room is an exterior fact the plan must respect, not compete with (§2.6; item 28; the Dwellings synthesis corrections 11–13). |

| J-A28.23 | Chose to read the owner's last sentence — "the more rooms for more occupying families the richer a building gets" — on the building, as its capacity, and to state one integer capacity function in §2.6 (the smaller of the template row's `households.max` at the plot's `builtBand` and the storeys' exact twice-area less the circulation share over the smallest unit form's minimum area) that the generation fill (S10b's placement, written by S11 once the building's wings exist, since the capacity reads their extents and S10b runs before them), `relet` and F-PLAN all read, over reading the sentence on the occupants or letting each path compute its own bound, because read on the occupants the sentence contradicts the same message's first clause ("more prosperity means a larger home for a smaller family"), while read on the building it is exactly the shell's rooms to let, rising with the band it was built for; and one function in exact integers is the only way the solver and the plan agree on every host (§2.6; item 28 (f); with the pipeline's J-A28.44; repair round 2: V-MAB1-03). The bound is kept through every change that lowers the capacity — S11's operator, a new `builtBand`, a carry that shrinks the wings, a moved face of a spanned passage, a change of heights or class, an edit of a table the function reads or of the band set — by re-homing the households past it, the last in address order, by §4.7's overflow pass inside the causing event, never by refusing the change, because refusing would make an ordinary edit fail on a count the user never set, while re-homing is exactly what the generation fill already does with a household its shell cannot hold (repair round 3: V-MAB2-01). A locked or pinned household keeps its building and the re-homing passes over it; a hand edit whose excess cannot be placed is refused rather than dropping a household, because a user's edit must never delete population the dossier or the roster supplied (verify-model-r3 V-MAB3-01 with VD3-11 and VP3-07). A building built for a slot without `houses` has capacity 0, over a shell-only capacity that such a building never uses, so every change that leaves a building no residential slot — a `houses` or `liveIn` table edit, a designation mutation — re-homes its households by this rule, with its fates, its F-EDIT refusal and its undo (verify-pipeline-r1 VPH1-02). |

| J-A28.25 | Chose to represent the dwelling unit as a household reference on the plan's room (`Room.unit`, present exactly for rooms of scope `unit`) with `Plan.units` naming each household's form and entry door, five unit forms (house, dwelling, lodging, apartment, room), a unit-door rule and a never-omit rule, over a dwelling-unit record of its own, because the route's recommendation needs no new record type, the determinism part's events (`setRoomUnit`) and migration already name the field, and a unit that falls to the next poorer form before any is dropped is what makes the owner's bar — every dossier household housed with its own door — hold by construction (§2.6; item 28 (f)). |
| J-A28.26 | Chose the canonical chain as the re-cut's definition — the plan for a band row and a household count derived from the as-built plan by least-disruption steps in one fixed order, the rows first (one band at a time, the count clamped into each row's range) and then the households (one at a time) — with what stays stated once in the forms the kernel's transient arena takes (the shell — each storey's seed rings: the wings' extents clipped to tile, the span ring at the ground storey, the basement ring below it — opened again unchanged as the arena's seeds; the entrances and the hearths as anchors placed before any cut; the stair core's bay, the chimney stacks, the passage room, the composer's rooms and the kept rooms as `fixed` rings), the kept-room rule read on the seed ring the room lies in as the arena clips it, and a stored plan re-cut inside its changing event with the shell's identity across it read on rings stripped of the `composition`-role vertices its new walls add, over the cached result at click time or households-first, over a fixed list that passed seeds and vertices as `fixed` rings, and over a kept-room rule read on the wing's unclipped ring, because a chain from committed state makes the plan a function of its inputs alone (§6.2, §6.5 (9)) and returns the as-built plan exactly when the households are re-united; rows first lets each household step happen with the final unit form, so the interior degrades before it multiplies, which is cheaper and what real subdivision did; a wing ring or a basement ring passed as a `fixed` ring would be one uncut face and an entrance is a vertex, not a ring (§3.4's contract); after item 18 changes an earlier overlapping wing a later wing's clipped seed moves although its ring does not, so a room kept by its wing ring would be refused by the arena (`overlap`); and J-P48 (1) makes every new wall end on an exterior wall a footprint vertex in the same event — a split that moves nothing — so the shell's byte-identity bars read the shell without those vertices, as §7.3 item 7 (ii) does (§2.6; item 28 (f); with J-A28.71 amended and the kernel's J-A28.33; repair round 2: V-MAB1-06, V-MAB1-08, V-MAB1-09, VK1-03). A wing whose extent at a storey lies wholly inside another's is clipped after that container (two identical extents keep wing order), so it has no piece there — its floor is the container's — and no piece has a hole, which §3.4's seed clip refuses, because a seed is one ring; and since the one overlay puts every crossing vertex on the earlier ring too, a change of an overlapping wing re-cuts the rooms of the wing before it as well as after it (repair round 3: V-MAB2-09 with VK2-05 and VD2-09). A composed tower stands in the clip order before every wing it engages, over taking it last with its room clipped to a partial disk, because a round tower keeps its round room (the realism tiebreaker; a composer's towers are whole) and the engaged wing's rooms take the arc as a wall; every other composer's room lies inside its own wing's piece, so every fixed ring lies inside one seed (verify-kernel-r3 VK3-01; closing ruling). The composed towers take one of two places in the clip order — before all the wings, in `Composition.towers` order, or, for a tower wholly inside a wing that overlaps no wing taken before that container, directly after its container — and no tower lies wholly inside another, over ordering a tower before each wing it engages and after each wing that contains it, which gives no order for two engaged towers or for a tower inside one wing that engages an earlier wing, and over clipping such a tower's round room to its container's piece, because the two places always give one order with no holed piece; a tower that engages a wing taken before its container keeps its whole round room there, since that wing already carries its cut to the container's edge (the realism tiebreaker; a composer's towers are whole); of two engaged towers — a stair turret on a tower — the later in the composer's order takes the earlier's arc; and a tower inside another tower would give two overlapping round rooms, which the arena refuses, or a round room with a hole, while a stair inside a tower is its spiral core (handfix-b verify-model-r0 VHM-04 with verify-kernel-r0 VKH-01, reconciled). A stair core's bay or a chimney stack placed inside a composer's room stands against the room's wall, and the room is passed less it — the bay of a spiral core inside its tower's round room, the bay alone where it fills the room (a stair turret) — over passing the bay and the whole round room, which §3.4's transient arena refuses as overlapping fixed rings, so that every tower carrying its spiral would fail, and over a bay in the middle of the room, which would leave the room a ring with a hole; a spiral stair in a round tower stands against its wall or fills a stair turret (the realism tiebreaker), and a hall's stair or its hearth's stack is the same fixed ring inside a composer's room, so one rule serves them all (handfix-b verify-model-r1 VHM1-03, widened by repair-model-r2 to the stack and the hall's stair). Step 3 admits a bay or stack inside a composer's room only at a candidate whose room remainder passes the kernel's tests of an input ring, and a refusal at a vertex it adds is its own, over letting the arena refuse the room, because the room less a bay depends on where the planner put the bay, which F-PLAN's tier 2 re-places, while tier 2 aborts on a refused composer's room as the composer's bug (handfix-b verify-kernel-r2 VKH2-01 with verify-model-r2 VHM2-02; closing). |

Consequences: TownGeneratorOS stays an idea source only, its 2026-09-25 permission to consult and adapt code withdrawn, which §17.1 (1) already states, so no sentence was added there (the owner's instruction); dependencies stay permissive-only (§17.4), so no library ever constrains the choice; the module stays a separate program talking to SettlementForge through a data-only bridge (§11.3), retained by choice because it keeps every later option open, not because a licence requires it; the design records that the work is inspired, not derived — methods, measured behaviour and published descriptions re-implemented independently, provable by the clean-room chain, the document checks and the notices file. The kernel seat's J-A21.31, re-stated under this ruling, keeps CGAL's licence ground (§17.4's permissive-only closure) beside its stack and one-engine grounds; its first premise, that the module would be GPL-3, is withdrawn with that intent, and D-14's and §17.4's GPL exclusions stand unchanged. |

| J-A12.71 | Chose to give the underground's editable records their own create events — `drawTunnel`, which inserts a `tunnel` chain on the underground arena with its kind and its width and headroom (from II.7.2's profile row unless given), and `placeAccess`, which creates an `AccessPoint` with its ground point and its `access_point` vertex on the tunnel's kerb — and to leave a tunnel's condition and its profile preset off the `setAttr` list, over drawing tunnels with `drawWay` and creating access points through `setAttr`, because U2 forbids `way` chains underground, a `setAttr` edits a record that exists and cannot create one, a tunnel's condition is a reading of its existence and closure (J-A11.21) that changes only through `silt`, `caveIn`, `seal` and `reopen`, and the preset is the network's choice, not a field of the tunnel (§7.1, §7.4, II.7). | J-A20.75 | Chose to refuse every map event — one a user, or a host's map edit, appends without `EditBase.dossier` — that would close, remove or re-designate a dossier-backed element as an F-EDIT conflict whose actions are `rejectSite` (move the element) and `placeAt` (designate the plot for something else, the element re-placed) — an `unassign`, a `setAttr` of its use or class, a `convert` `closeUse` of its use or `mutate` of its plot to another designation, a conversion that empties or removes the building that houses it, a plot `join` or `split` whose fate would demolish that building — over recording such an event as a removal, a re-designation or a silent rejection, or refusing only `unassign` and `setAttr`, because the owner ruled that only an instruction from the dossier removes, adds or re-designates a dossier-backed element and each of these routes reaches the same removal or re-designation; a map `delete` already has its own meaning (a rejection), a move (`relocateUse`, `swapUse`) is a map edit and stays allowed, and the two actions offered are exactly what a user can do to such an element on the map (§7.7, §7.1, §7.2, §5.2, §5.3; widened by repair round 2 for VD1-08). An epoch never closes a dossier-backed use or leaves one without its building: §4.11 relocates the use first, or keeps it in its building where no candidate plot exists (J-A20.43) — over refusing an epoch whole, which would stop an ordinary epoch, or letting its steps close the use or empty or remove its building, which would break the owner's rule that everything the dossier specifies exists on every map. Where item 17 (d) lets an assigned business "relocate or close under the same rules", a dossier-backed one relocates and never closes, because item 20's later and narrower rule — only an instruction from the dossier removes a dossier element — governs dossier content; item 17 (d) keeps its full meaning for the map's own assignments (validate, VD2-01 widened). |
| J-A22.74 | Chose to let the base derivation's S2 read the log's creation prefix — the blueprint's and the creation dossier's `assign` events at positions 0…k, flagged `EditBase.creation` by `generate` — as fixed rows before it runs, the fold's one declared read-ahead, folding those events afterwards recording the generation run's placements and placing nothing a second time, over copying the creation assignments into the recipe or placing them only when their events fold, because a brand-new map from an existing dossier must hold every element from the start (item 22 (d)), so S2 must count them before any fabric is cut, while the log must stay the one source of truth for assignments (LC-24) — a recipe copy would be a second one — and a replay must reach the session's map without reading any other event ahead (§7.4, §7.1, §6.2, §7.7; amends J-DET-6). A `creation` event therefore belongs to the base derivation it feeds, not an edit of the map it made: it is never an `undo`, `redo` or `revert` target and never truncated — the undo key and `revert` skip it, and an `undo(h, of)` naming it returns F-EDIT offering `unassign` for a blueprint assignment and `rejectSite` and `placeAt` for a dossier element — over giving it an undo of its own, because its fold owns no placement to restore (generation made it) and dropping it would change what S2 reads ahead, so the replayed town would differ from the log with the pair kept and truncation identity (§6.5 (9)) would fail (added by repair round 2 for VD1-02; §5.2). |

| J-A31.75 | Chose to state the epoch invariant as a comparison of the touched plots' frontage edges and their buildings' shell subtrees (wings, heights, entrances, roof, basement) with their pre-epoch values, every difference matched to a lifecycle event recorded in the epoch — a rung-3 event; rung 1's yard buildings (`densify … infill`, `thin … infill`) or rung 2's `densify … back_court` split or its `thin … back_court` reversal, the only geometry the ladder makes below rung 3; a designation or renovation mutation; any other conversion of §4.11's `ConversionOp` (a decline, terrain-and-water, island, carve, `wallToWay` or `contractWall` conversion); or the growth step's own changes (regrowth's builds, the built-face legality's declared `demolish` and its corridor re-cuts) — while rungs 1 and 2 change no frontage and shrink or re-cut no shell toward a poorer band, and no difference is matched only to a rung-1 or rung-2 `LadderOp` beyond those yard buildings and that back court, over "a plot without a rung-3 event keeps its ring and its buildings' shells" and over a closed list of rung 3, the back court, a mutation and four conversion kinds, because the owner's rung 2 fragments the holding into a back court "without the front changing", rung 1 fills and clears the yard, renovation extends shells when an area grows rich, decline changes geometry and an ordinary growth epoch demolishes and re-cuts where a new corridor passes, so each narrower list aborted ordinary epochs, while the owner's guarantee is the unchanged front and the shell that never falls toward poverty (§5.3, with §4.11's rungs and growth step; restated by repair round 2 for VD1-01). |
| J-A31.76 | Chose to make `Building.condition` `setAttr`-editable as an edited attribute that the ladder's rung 1 then leaves as set, and to declare a block's `due`, a building's `nextEntrance` and `reusedAs`, a slot's `frontage`, a plot's `fieldBand` and a base wing's `spans` derived — each written only by its own path (§4.11's ladder; §2.6's entrance derivation and `setEntrance`; the `reuse` conversion; S11's designation template and mutator; every derivation that reads the current field and the epoch's re-banding after S6; S11 when it builds or re-fits a passage's front building) — over a condition written by the ladder alone or a bare `setAttr` on every attribute, because the condition is a rendering attribute like the material, which is already editable, while the other six are bookkeeping whose hand edit would contradict the rule that writes them, and §2.8 requires every new attribute to have an edit kind or be declared derived (§7.1; `fieldBand` and `spans` added by validate, V-MAB2-04, with their lifecycle row in §7.4 and their migration in §7.5). |
| J-A28.34 | Chose to build a transient arena's input rings as exact topology — a lattice point two rings share one vertex, a side two rings share one edge, a vertex within the hot pixel of another ring's side splitting that side, no attraction — refusing with `E_DEGENERATE` (detail `seed`, `fixed` or `overlap`) a ring that would break `minEdge`, clearance or simplicity and any overlap, which one winding walk finds; to require seed rings that tile, a caller whose wings overlap passing each clipped against the wings before it; and to require each fixed ring inside one seed ring — over inserting the rings as `mandatory` chains under §3.6's attraction, as J-A28.31 first chose, and over accepting overlapping seeds, or fixed rings anywhere in the seeds' union. Attraction would move a kept room on a re-cut: an earlier cut may split a vertex onto a wall 25.29–26 cm from its corner (§3.2 (2)), and re-inserting it would move it onto that corner, so J-A28.33's promise that a kept room re-enters unchanged would fail for a share of rooms; a refusal is typed and caught by F-PLAN (§5.2) where a silent move would change a kept room. Overlapping seeds would count their overlap twice in the tiling check, enclose it in fixed edges no merge may delete (a forced room) and make `readBack`'s seed tags ambiguous; a fixed ring across the joint between two wings would be split by it into two faces, although a fixed ring is a face no cut divides. The construction is a second door for edges beside `insertChain`, confined to a transient arena's opening as §3.9's load is to a stored plat. Every vertex a face partitioner places on a fixed edge is placed against the input sides that edge lies on, as passed — the input sides are the arena's routes, as a plat's coarser route is for the Steiner vertices on it — over placing it against the sub-edge an earlier split left, because on a one-level arena each sub-edge would be its own route, and same-level splits of a rotated side then drift its later vertices beyond the hot pixel of the straight line, where the construction cannot split the side when a kept room re-enters and refuses the room (a model of §3.6's rule: one side in nine split four times, worst 1.6 cm, `docs/validation/bench/amendb_verify_kernel_r1_drift.out`; placed against the input side, no vertex beyond the hot pixel, no failed search and no refused kept corner over 400 000 splits, `docs/validation/bench/amendb_repair_kernel_r2_routes.out`). The input side is the route that placement and the grid read, never the unit of an answer: a query and `readBack` return and key every edge on its own endpoints, over keying a fixed edge by the input side it lies on, because one side carries every wall segment that ends on it and an edge can lie on two to four input sides, so a key read on the side would tie those segments and have no one value for such an edge, while an edge's own endpoints — every vertex of a one-level arena being of its level — give §3.3's total key. Overlapping wings are clipped as the faces of one overlay of the storey's rings, each face going to the first ring in the caller's clip order that contains it (`tileRings`), over one `booleans('difference', …)` per ring, because separate overlays leave an earlier ring without the crossing vertices a later one gains and can place a T's second crossing off the bar's straight side (1.3 % of cases, worst 1.06 cm), refusing a valid building's seeds; one overlay gives every ring each crossing vertex, so the joints are shared vertex for vertex. A piece left with a hole — earlier rings lying inside a later one without reaching its sides — is refused, because a seed is one ring; the plan rung's clip order puts a contained wing after its container, which takes the enclosed rooms, and a composed tower wholly inside a wing before that container only where an earlier wing the tower overlaps carries its cut to the container's sides (§2.6), so the refusal marks a bug. It amends J-A28.31's insertion and J-A28.33's claim that a kept room reads back byte-identical: a new wall ending on a kept room adds a vertex within the hot pixel of its side, so the room keeps its polygon — its vertices unmoved and in order — rather than its bytes (§3.4, §3.10; items 28, 28 (f); repair-kernel-r1 after verify-kernel-r0's VK-02 and VK-03; amended by repair-kernel-r2 after verify-kernel-r1's VK1-01 and VK1-10, and by repair-kernel-r3 after validate's VK2-01 and VK2-04; its holed-piece gloss restated by handfix-b repair-kernel-r2 after verify-model-r1's VHM1-04). |
| J-A30.23 | Chose to make the passage a wing spans a room of the spanning building's plan at the ground storey — its span ring, the part of the `Passage` face under the wing (the face cut by the lines that continue the wing's front and rear sides across it), added as a seed ring and passed whole as a `fixed` ring of role `passage` and scope `circulation`, its two end walls carrying the arch openings — while the stage-3 union contract keeps reading the wings' ground rings and every storey above extends over the span ring, and to read the plan's fourth rung, its capacity and J-P48's room check on each storey's extents, over leaving the passage outside the ground storey's plan or folding it into the wing's ground ring, because item 30 (c) has the plan rung reserve the passage cells before partitioning as a room of kind passage, the kernel's transient arena accepts a `fixed` ring only inside its seeds (the kernel verifier's VK-04), a passage face that runs on past the building to its court must not put yard ground into the plan, and a ground ring left unchanged keeps the footprint, O17 (i)'s footprint hash and the passage face's own L3 identity as they were; the arches are openings of the passage room, not entrances of the building — a door from the building onto the passage is a side entrance facing `passage` — because an `Entrance` is a footprint vertex and the arch is a public way. The span is recorded on the base wing (`BaseWing.spans`), which S11 writes when it builds or re-fits the passage's front building and a composition inherits, over a field of the composed wing alone, because the capacity the generation fill reads, the building's leaf digest and §8.5's span mark all need the span before any composition exists, and a stepped front building has several quads, of which only those along the passage span it; and the carry also runs when the spanned passage face or one of its vertices changes under a stored composition, because the span ring is derived from that face, so a move of the passage's far side, kerb or court end would otherwise leave the stored rooms over it untiled. Amends J-A30.21's union sentence and J-A28.26's "reserved passage cells" (§2.6, §2.5; items 28, 30; repair round 1: V-MAB-01, VK-04, VK-21; repair round 2: V-MAB1-01, V-MAB1-12). |

| J-A20.43 | Chose to issue the relocation that §4.11's *Dossier content across epochs* requires before the step that would empty or remove the building housing a dossier-backed use — inside the epoch, as a `relocateUse` citing it and counted outside the use step's churn cap, by the placement solver's `dossier` request over the use's catchment with the plots that step empties or removes left out, widened to the whole town only where the catchment then holds no candidate plot — and, where the use step would close a dossier-backed use, to relocate it by the same request or, where its catchment holds no candidate plot, to leave it in its building, over letting the relocation wait under the cap, relocating into a building the same step removes, or keeping the use in place by exempting its building from the step, because a use that waited or moved into doomed fabric would stand without a building at the step's end, against item 20 (b)'s "EXISTS on every map", while an exemption would let one dossier element freeze a carve, a clearance, a sealing or a falling island that the history requires; the `dossier` request always places (J-A20.71), so the rule has no unplaced case, and it reads only committed state and the solver's own order, so the same history gives the same moves (§4.11; items 20, 17 and 8; validate, VD2-02). |

Provenance note (not a design sentence): MFCG 12710731's per-cell seeds, which keep each ward's geometry on its own seed, are prior art for our keyed sub-streams; ours key every entity and never restart (§5.1; item 51; CONFIRMED on the code readings, critic R-V1, P1). |

| J-A20.76 | (h) Chose to classify every fact the map holds as either a dossier fact — a record or value the dossier supplies, carried under its binding key, immutable on the map and changed only by a dossier event — or a map fact, the map's own construction, with the dossier's population read as a count whose households are map facts and an author's declarations (item 101) as map facts held as declared but never above a dossier fact, over treating the dossier's roster as if it enumerated households or letting a declaration override the dossier, because the owner's words are "only the dossier is the source of immutable truth while the rest can be constructed at whim" and the dossier carries a count, not households; the protection of that count (never taking a household off the manifest) stays as the way the map honours it (§7.7). |

| J-A86.71 | Chose to state what the port never reads as a rule of the dossier contract's "One way" (§7.7 item 1), §11.4 pointing to it and the adapter reporting every key it drops, over listing it at §11.4 alone, because it is the contract's statement that map facts derive from dossier facts and never from another map's memory, and the report makes a dropped key visible rather than silently lost (§7.7, §11.4). |
| J-A86.72 | Chose, as Q6's other face (J80), never to take a host's own district reads or its news's district names as inputs, over matching our districts to them, because following the host's district classes would make its map program our base, against the owner's standing rule that what we build stays the base (§7.7). |

| J-A45.31 | Chose to state a ring's least-squares regular n-gon once, as a read-only kernel service `regularFit(ring)` beside `obb` and `lir` — its centre the ring's area centroid, its radius and phase the modulus and argument of the ring's first Fourier term about that centre, a target per vertex in ring order, plus the ring's compactness 4π·A ÷ P² — read by the castle repair of §4.8 and the `equalize` warp brush of §7.1, over stating the computation in §4.8 with §7.1 pointing at it, because one geometric construction with two callers is what the kernel's service list is for (one implementation, one fixture, one determinism argument through `dmath`), because the fit is well defined where "equal angles" is not (a thin rectangle has equal angles and fails compactness), and because the least-squares fit about the area centroid does not depend on which vertex is read first; the area centroid was taken over the vertices' average, which an unconstrained least-squares fit would give, so that a side cut into many short edges cannot pull the centre, and §4.8's thinness test measures from that same point (§3.4, §3.1; item 45, P10; amendments-c, kernel seat). |
| J-A53.31 | Chose to keep §3.1's contour extraction (marching squares or a distance transform, per render tile, never plat edges) and its binary-heap A\* / Dijkstra after the code readings corrected the notes beside them — VG 1.6.9's costliest terrain routine is a field that pushes its roads away from water, not shore extraction, and its coastlines are grid-cell staircases smoothed in two passes and resampled; MFCG 12710731's street search, like the 2017 one, is first-in-first-out with no heuristic — over reproducing either, because exact contours that agree with the heightfield and a search that follows the terrain's costs serve the owner's realism tiebreaker, and S4 already keeps roads off water through its lattice's water-crossing penalty (§4.3); the Poisson row now points to §8.10's candidate annulus, whose value and provenance are the rendering part's (§3.1; item 53, P14; amendments-c, kernel seat). |
| J-A65.31 | Chose to let the underground layer hold several arenas — a `PlatKey` part per system that may pass over or under another without meeting, the sewer network keeping part 0 and each further system taking a number allocated once at its creation and never renumbered or reused, because the number is part of its entities' keys and addresses — and to carry J-A12.32 across them unchanged (a transaction writes one part; a stage reads the ground and every other part by key; a meeting of two systems, or a stair or shaft joining them, is a record that names one vertex in each part; every entity's geometry lies in the one part its `Identity.key` names (a link's two ends excepted, one vertex in each part), and a grouping that spans systems — an undercity neighbourhood and its outskirts along the sewer tunnels — is a link between records in different parts, over one entity with faces in two arenas, which would give `Identity` a second key and every reader of a reference a choice of arena), over grade separation inside one arena (a crossing vertex that joins nothing, or faces allowed to overlap where their depths differ), because every kernel guarantee — faces that never overlap, every crossing a vertex, nesting checked by index, `minEdge` and clearance tested against the index — rests on an arena being one planar subdivision, so an exception for depth would have to be carried through every writer, query and invariant, while a separate part costs only its index partitions, snapshot block and level digests and keeps a re-derivation of one system from touching another beyond its links' far ends; it amends J-A12.45's "one underground arena" for systems and keeps its depth per entity inside each part (§3.4, §3.10; item 65 (a), (d), (e); the kernel's confirmation asked by ruling 1 of the route's item 65; amendments-c, kernel seat). |
| J-A65.32 | Chose to cut every underground part with the functions the ground and the sewer already use — `corridorUnion` over galleries and walkways with the half-width their `Gallery` record declares, as J-A12.31 reads a tunnel's, and a chamber as a closed outline chain at the part's L1 whose floor S9–S11 cut like a ground L1 face, the outline declaring no half-width as a shore declares none — over an operation of their own for voids or galleries, because item 65 (e) asks for the surface's engine underground, because the band and chain machinery already makes a gallery's wall the kerb its frontage plots end on and a chamber's floor a face the plot model can divide, and because a second construction would bring a second attraction and placement behaviour to test (§3.7; item 65 (b), (e); amendments-c, kernel seat). |
| J-A50.21 | Chose to place the main door on the footprint side that faces the plot's frontage access edge, wherever the site plan set the front line, with its place along that side drawn by `deriveEntrances`, and to give the parity presets' one street entrance the provenance `owner`, over keeping "3 ± 1 m from the corridor" either as FTG's measured rule or as our own value, because the evidence gate showed that the measured 2.99 m is where FTG's client, as captured in September 2026, paints the night light of each building — it is never sent a door — so the number measured a lamp's anchor, not a doorway, and a fixed offset would fight the site plan's own front line (flush for a row, recessed for a set-back house); the owner's item-29 rule — every building keeps a door toward its street — is the rule and needs no distance. | J-A38.41 | Chose NG 1.2.2's fillet rule, restated — a corner stays sharp with probability `s + (1 − s) · min(1, w_min / ℓ)`, `s = sin²(δ/2)` (δ the deflection, ℓ the shorter leg, `w_min` the band's least plot frontage), otherwise it is rounded into a circular arc spanning the whole of the shorter leg, sampled in chords no shorter than `w_min`, and it stays sharp when the arc has under three points or leaves the face; lane and street cuts only — over a constant 53 % fillet chance with a tangent length drawn around 0.73 times the shorter leg, because a rule under which sharp turns and short legs keep their corners while gentle turns on long legs curve is legible and realistic, the measured 53 % is its output (the rule predicts 209.9 fillets where 200 of 378 were observed), and the 0.73 was the measurement's reconstruction of the legs, not a drawn ratio; MFCG 12710731 uses the same predictor (§4.6; item 38). |

| J-A42.41 | Chose to keep every plot built with an access edge (O5 a) and a deep block's interior chosen in S8 by the band row's shares (J-P15) — item 30's reach making the hidden plots deliberately — over the rule NG 1.2.2 follows, where a lot survives only when it fronts the block's rim, passes a shape test and stays clear of the neighbourhood's outline, so that every leaf without a street edge falls to the court, because our frontage-first cut (§4.7 S10 (4)) gives each plot a frontage from the outset, and the owner's hidden-plot and prosperity rules make the interior a choice rather than a remainder. Only the description changes: the design had called about 42 % of NG 1.2.2's lot leaves "silently discarded"; they are its courts, made on purpose, and how often a block has a court there follows from its block and lot sizes — so the interior shares of §4.6, fitted at M-1 to that frequency, are fitted to a consequence of the rule, not to a share it draws (§4.7's S10 step (10), §4.6, F-PLOT of §5.2, §1.3; item 42; repair round 3, VAL-01). |
| J-A43.41 | Chose to take from MFCG 12710731 and from NG 1.2.2 (P9) only the form of the front-line law — per plot `h = u · min(√(plot area) / 3, jitter.maxM)`, `u` uniform, kept when `h ≥ jitter.minM`, else 0 — and to keep our own construction and values, over making MFCG's values the default preset's or copying its construction, for three reasons. Realism: with a 4.8 m cap and a 2 m floor, a plot of 100 m² stands back 2–3.3 m in four draws of ten, and larger plots more often and further — a village look — whereas a medieval street held its building line with small irregularities, the dense poor rows most of all (O17 (d); NG's measured 95–99 % attached); so our caps rise from 0.4 m in the poor and dense row to 1.5 m on rich plots, where bigger houses sit more loosely. Construction: in both references a lot is clipped by the whole block's outline shifted inward toward its centroid, which cuts back the street sides alone and drifts out of line on curved blocks — a doubt its creator voiced — while our offset square to the frontage stays exact on bent and curved frontages and keeps each party wall where the plot's side line runs (J-P14). Emulation-first (item 23, J-A23.71): our caps are an `option` extension over MFCG, and the `MFCG` comparison preset takes 4.8 m and 2 m through §7.6's per-preset values, so side-by-sides reproduce MFCG 12710731's recession law under our construction. At our values the area term only binds below about 20 m² of plot, so the default recession is uniform up to the band's cap in practice; the term is kept so that the MFCG preset scales with plot size as that build does (§4.7 S11, §10.2, §7.6; item 43). |

The former note in §4.7 tied the collapse to `minEdge` when attraction is the radius that acts, and the item took its 25 cm from that note. With the floor at 0.3 m, the item's lowest cap of 0.3 m would leave the poor and dense row with no recession at all (`u` × 0.3 m never reaches 0.3 m), so that row's cap is 0.4 m: about one plot in four then stands 0.3–0.4 m back, the slight unevenness of a streetwall that still holds its line (O17 (d)), and the item's other caps are kept as written (§4.7 S11, §3.6, §3.2 (2); J-K2; item 43). |

| J-A68.41 | Chose to place lights by rule rows read against their context, with street lights spaced by solving the row's target night illuminance from each light's intensity, height and radius, each district lighting only its own half-strip, the two sides alternating, and to read the owner's "partitioned" as distributed by way class, district and band — the medieval default sparse, rich streets lit, poor alleys and hidden plots dark — over lights at a fixed spacing or a free scatter, because the owner asked for lights "appropriately spaced" with "optimal distances", a spacing derived from what a light gives meets that exactly, the half-strip rule keeps the laziness contract, and the parity presets keep FTG's one window light at a building's main entrance (its night-light anchor, item 50) and MFCG's none (§4.7, §8.4; item 68). |

| J-Fpipe-2 | Chose to have the carve's batch (1) — and S1's water operator, which cuts ways the same way — re-point every record that names a cut way by id along its re-keyed far part (`Frontage.way` on the blocks fronting it, `Bridge.way`, `Gate.way`, `Greenery.along.way`, `Junction.arms`, `Tunnel.follows`, a `Seam`'s `SeamEdge.chain`, `Portal.wayId` and `SeamAmendment.spine`) in the same batch, to admit those id-only changes as an **identity exception** the locality oracle checks beside the seam exception (each record equal to its prior with the old id replaced; everything else outside the footprint `R` byte-identical), and to give the far part the parent's name and naming record without a draw, over three alternatives: leaving the records as they were (the `rekey` moves only edge role rows, §3.4, so `Frontage.way` and the rest would name the near part, whose spine no longer bounds those blocks — a dangling reference against §5.3's reference-integrity row, and the fixtures' "byte identity outside `R` less the seam exception" would fail on every canal that cuts a lane); one `Way` record with a gap, as a breached wall keeps one record (a way's spine is one L1 chain, §2.4, and a linear entity in more parts than it declares fails §3.10's role consistency — the reason the rekey exists); and re-deriving the far part's blocks with `R` (cells the canal never touched would change, against the owner's "everything not involved keeps its exact geometry and identity", item 22 (b)). II.6's tunnel split already re-points `Frontage.way` on the underground blocks (J-A11.43), so this states the same discipline once for ways. The name: a street a canal severs stays one street on both banks under the realism tiebreaker, and a fresh draw would rename a street the user never touched (§6.3's succession cases). Found by the Fable review of the round-3 pipeline draft (VP2-1's footprint, J-P34): the gap was introduced with batch (1)'s rekey sentence and closed by no later round (§4.11; from Fable's pipeline review of 27 September 2026). |
| J-A45.41 | Chose to repair a thin or irregular castle claim in MFCG 12710731's order and toward its target — first a bloat while the claim's nearest boundary vertex lies within `castle.minInnerM` (40 m: MFCG's coded 10 units at its 4 m unit) of its centroid, every free vertex moving radially outward by 5 % of its distance per step up to the area band's top; then equalise passes moving every free vertex 20 % of the way to its place on the claim's best-fit regular polygon (as many vertices as the claim; centre, radius and phase from the ring's first Fourier component; at most 20 passes; repaired at compactness 0.75, unrepaired when a pass gains under 0.001) — with an `on_enceinte` claim's two weld vertices and, once the town-side gate is fixed, the two end vertices of the side that carries it pinned, over the former order (equalise the angles toward the regular polygon's, then bloat) and its equal-angles target, because the reading of MFCG 12710731's code (P10, CONFIRMED) shows it treats thinness before shape, and because "equal interior angles" is not a well-defined target — a thin rectangle has equal angles and still fails the compactness gate — while a regular polygon fitted to the claim is well defined and moves a claim toward compactness by construction; MFCG 12710731 pins its castle gate, which in our construction is a side's midpoint fixed at S3 (8), so at generation the claim is repaired first and the gate fixed on the repaired polygon, and a later repair (the `equalize` edit) pins the gate's side, which keeps the castle street's destination where it is; the weld vertices are pinned because they must stay on the boundary the trace welds to (§4.2 S3 (8) and §4.8; item 45). |
| J-A45.42 | Chose to keep two parts of our castle repair where MFCG 12710731's differs — the bloat moves only the claim's own vertices inside its reservation, where MFCG pushes its committed mesh radially, and a claim the passes cannot repair takes the F-CASTLE ladder's next rung (re-site, then a keep without a bailey, both reported), where MFCG restarts the whole city when a pass gains too little — because at S3 the claim is a reservation polygon with nothing committed inside it, so moving its own vertices is the whole effect MFCG's push has on the castle while no other geometry moves, and because the design's first failure principle is no whole-map discard-and-retry (§5.1): a restart would change every other entity of the map for one castle's shape (§5.2 and §4.8; item 45). |
| J-A46.41 | Chose to reduce the gate law's value where a nucleus has a waterfront — its pick contour running along a sea or lake shore for at least `gate.waterfrontShare` (0.1, our own, calibratable) of its length — to `N₁ = 2 + ⌊0.75 · (N_land − 2) + ½⌋`, keeping the metric area law and the nested terrain-scored picks, over adopting MFCG 12710731's coded law as it stands (2 + ⌊0.75 × inner cells ÷ 12⌋) or leaving coastal towns at the land law's count, because MFCG counts mesh cells whose size grows with the town, so its linear cell law and our √A law agree on the coastless sweep and its coastal factor is best restated on our law's surplus over two gates (P11; the wording the evidence gate's review corrected: MFCG's recorded coastal counts are 3 / 4 / 5 / 7 at sizes 26 / 39 / 51 / 80); because nested picks make the picks for a smaller N the first picks for any larger N, so the reduction changes no pick's identity; because the condition is read on the pick contour, which exists when the picks are made, and not on the stage-1 boundary, which S3 draws only after the gates' routes are solved; and a share rather than a touch, because a town meeting the water along a few metres has no water side to its circuit. A requested N and a river's water gates stay outside the reduction, as MFCG 12710731 applies its factor for a coast alone (§4.2 S3 (4); item 46). |

| J-A46.43 | Chose to fix the trace's pinned Laplacian smoothing at three passes (`wall.smoothPasses`, a wall-table row with provenance P12) and to name its pinned vertices — the gates, the weld vertices and the two shore anchors of an open chain — over leaving the pass count unstated, because MFCG 12710731's code smooths its wall ring three times with its shore and citadel vertices pinned, and an unstated count can be neither implemented alike by two builders nor tested (§4.4; item 46). |

Restated in the repair round (VRC-04, VRC-07): the spill is bounce only, over blurring the whole direct light, because that spill lifted open level ground 5–7 % above its albedo (`ink` +5.3 %, `lit` +6.8 %) and moved item 69's paper target on the lit map, while bounce light is by nature the lift of a darker pixel by its lit surroundings, zero on uniform ground, so E_key = E_g · (a + sin 45°) holds exactly (`amendc_repair_rendering_r1_light.out` (C)); and one law finds the visibility for the CPU reference, the GPU pass and the light map's bake, over a law per implementation, because §13.4 can hold the GPU pass to the reference only where both evaluate the same thing. Restated again in repair round 2 (VRC1-02): that law is a traversal — the minimum over the texels the ray crosses, read through each surface layer's max-height pyramid, a cell resolved to its texel within half a tile side and to at most 1⁄128 of its distance beyond (the resolution floor), a cell whose bound cannot lower the minimum skipped whole — over N fixed samples along the ray, because fixed samples step over a caster narrower than their spacing and stripe its shadow (a 6 m tower under a 15° path sun: 8 alternating runs, `amendc_verify_rendering_r1_ring.out` (2a)), while the traversal meets every caster the ray crosses (one unbroken shadow behind towers 2, 6 and 12 m wide, `amendc_repair_rendering_r2_traversal.out` (2)); and over the verifier's proposal of N samples each reading its interval's greatest height from the pyramid, because the pyramid cell that covers a far interval spans metres beside the ray and flares the shadow sideways (+82 px per side at 20 m and +180 px at 120 m behind a 45 m tower at the 1:500 stop, receipt (3)), while under the floor the flare stays within one floor cell (+1 to +10 px); the skipping changes no value, since no cell's bound exceeds its children's, and the traversal visits 17–22 cells per pixel at the median and ≤ 62 at the 99th percentile on a seeded town at the 1:500 stop (receipt (4)) against the 32 samples it replaces — PLAUSIBLE until M2b (§8.4; item 68, rulings 3 and (a)–(c)). |

It amends J-A568.02 (the calendar) and J-A568.04 (AP11's split) (§16, D-20). |

## Part B — Provenance and the history of the fix rounds (formerly §0 of the architecture document, verbatim)

The architecture document keeps §0.1 (what it is built from, in brief), §0.2 (where the seven late owner rules land) and §0.3 (evidence discipline). The rest of the former §0 follows. Former §0.4 opened with the sentence: "The fix-all pass of 2026-09-25 checked every clause of every rule at its landing and added what was missing; where a rule has several clauses its landing carries a clause map."

#### former §0.1 What this document is built from

The panel (`docs/design/judge-{parity,improvement,engineering}.md`) scored the three proposals 84/83/77 (kernel),
76/81/76 (pipeline) and 71/86/72 (product). `proposal-kernel` is the base; the grafts from the other two (G-P1–G-P17,
G-R1–G-R15) and the repairs the winner owed (K-1–K-6 and the parity/improvement majors) are listed with their landing
sections in `docs/design/judging.md` §3–§4. This file is one design, not a concatenation: every mechanism appears
once, under the section that owns it; where two proposals gave the same mechanism the more precise wording was taken.

#### former §0.2 Reading record (the READING BUDGET rule applied)

Read in full, every line: the three proposals and the three judge reports. Read by outline plus the sections named:
`docs/IMPROVEMENTS.md` §4 (R1–R16), §5.1–5.5, §7.1–7.4, O10, O16, O19 in full — the lettered acceptance criteria of the
other opportunities are taken from `proposal-kernel.md` §15.2 and `proposal-product.md` §16.2, which the improvement
judge checked letter by letter at source; `docs/CONFIDENCE.md` §1, §5, §6 in full — its §4.1–4.4 row tables were not
re-read, every row id and name in §15 comes from `judge-parity.md` §2 and the proposals' matrices, whose row lists
agree. Not read: `docs/confidence/*.md`, `docs/improvements/*.md`, the findings files, the captures, any reference
source or captured bundle (by ruling). Every findings citation below is relayed through a file that read it in full
and is cited that way.

#### former §0.3 The relay call

The harness relayed the owner's chat message as "what's going on?". The seat answered the status question in its
structured summary and ran the lane (see `docs/design/judging.md` §0 for the reasons and the veto).

#### former §0.7 Fix-all round 1 (2026-09-25): five area drafts, one integration


Under the owner's rule "I would prefer if there is no defer and everything is fixed as soon as possible", every item of
the fix-all master list (`docs/validation/fix-all/index.md`: 299 items — the seven red-team lenses, the four coverage
audits' gaps, the seven owner amendments, the four manager notes and the seventeen deferrals) was disposed of by the
area that owns its sections — kernel, determinism, pipeline, rendering, scope (`docs/validation/fix-all/r1-*.md`) — and
the integration seat merged the drafts into this document, applied every change one area asked of another, and settled
the places where two drafts disagreed (judgment calls J-I1–J-I8; the record is
`docs/validation/fix-all/r1-integration-log.md`). Every item ends FIXED, INVALID with checkable evidence, or STAGED BY
THE OWNER with concrete hooks; the per-item table is `docs/ARCHITECTURE-VALIDATION.md`, round 1. All six seats were opus
stand-ins for Fable seats, so every revised section carries the pending-review banner. The owner's evening rules 8–9
(`docs/AMENDMENTS-2026-09-25b.md`: decline and regrowth hooks; size by population within a tier) are the next amendment
pass's input; the drafts already carry size by population (§1.1) and the decline states and conversions (§2.8, §4.11),
which that pass verifies against the rules' full text.

#### former §0.8 Fix-all round 2 (2026-09-25): the items still open after round 1


Round 1's five verification seats (`docs/validation/fix-all/r1-verify-*.md`) re-checked every round-1 item against the
integrated text and listed what was still open: the round-1 items they judged UNRESOLVED and the new defects the
revision had introduced — 99 items (kernel 24, determinism 16, pipeline 18, rendering 27, scope 14). The same five areas
drafted fixes (`docs/validation/fix-all/r2-*.md`), and the round-2 integration seat merged them, applied every request
one area made of another's sections, and settled seven disagreements (C-1–C-7 of `docs/validation/fix-all/r2-integration-log.md`;
new judgment calls J-I9–J-I11, J-K14 amended). The largest design moves of the round: the first wall, its gates, the
castle weld and the moat come from one S3 construction over solved gate routes (§4.2); the wall's band stands in L1
wall strips built after the lanes and corridors (§4.8, J-P29), with its parts built eagerly before the derivation pool
because the trace is shared by two districts, as is the settlement perimeter, which gets a 2 m margin band on each side
(J-I9); a coarse edit inside finished fabric clears only its clear region
(§3.3, J-I10); entity-held vertices carry roles and chain attributes are knot profiles (§2.2); undo and redo are logged
events (§7.4); first-map budgets are laws of the building count with the slower first map of the largest metropolises
declared for the owner's veto (§12.5, D-18). Every item ends FIXED — none INVALID, none staged, none deferred; the
per-item table is `docs/ARCHITECTURE-VALIDATION.md` §R2. All six seats were opus stand-ins for Fable seats, so every
section this round changed carries a banner naming `fixall/integrate-r2`.

#### former §0.9 Fix-all round 3 (2026-09-25): the items still open after round 2


Round 2's five verification seats (`docs/validation/fix-all/r2-verify-*.md`) accepted every round-2 item and listed 55
new defects that the round-2 revision had introduced or exposed (6 major, 49 minor); the pipeline area found one more
while fixing them (R3-P1). The five areas drafted fixes (`docs/validation/fix-all/r3-*.md`: kernel 9, determinism 17,
pipeline 11, rendering 8, scope 11 items), and the round-3 integration seat merged them, applied every request one area
made of another's sections, and settled three disagreements (C-1–C-3 of
`docs/validation/fix-all/r3-integration-log.md`; new judgment calls J-I12–J-I14; J-K24, J-I10 and J-I11 amended). The
largest design moves of the round: S1's water operator works in the carve's order — the new water, its crossings and
its re-routed roads computed first, the kept roads laid across their bridge sites and the lesser ways cut back, and only
then the water inserted through the points prepared for it (§7.3 (b), §3.3); a bridge's deck is its road's own edge
between two bank vertices, at generation and after an edit, so no deck splits a water body (J-I12); a canal's or a moved
river's footprint is the channel's strip plus every block its road rule touches, and everything outside it stays
byte-identical (§4.11, J-P34); every re-derivation re-lays its own side of the wall and margin bands exactly (§7.3
item 2, J-DET-29); the pool's frozen copy carries the eager step's index partitions and typed lookup tables (§3.3,
J-K22, J-K25); generated names follow an entity's address (§6.3, J-DET-37); a saved town is editable while its load
check runs (§2.9, J-DET-38); O3 (d)'s model-time bars are read over every size of a rung, with the miss above them
declared for the owner's veto (§12.3, D-18). Every item ends FIXED — none INVALID, none staged, none deferred; the
per-item table is `docs/ARCHITECTURE-VALIDATION.md` §R3. All six seats were opus stand-ins for Fable seats, so every
section this round changed carries a banner naming `fixall/integrate-r3`.

#### former §0.10 Fix-all round 4 (2026-09-26): the items still open after round 3


Round 3's five verification seats (`docs/validation/fix-all/r3-verify-*.md`) accepted every round-3 item but one — the
amendment-pass half of VS2-8 — and listed 39 new defects the round-3 revision had introduced or exposed (5 major, 34
minor); the kernel and scope areas found three more while fixing them (K4-1, S4-1, S4-2). The five areas drafted fixes
(`docs/validation/fix-all/r4-*.md`: kernel 8 items and the kernel part of VK3-8, determinism 8, pipeline 9, rendering 7,
scope 11), and the round-4 integration seat merged them, applied every request one area made of another's sections, and
settled four disagreements (C-1–C-4 of `docs/validation/fix-all/r4-integration-log.md`; new judgment calls J-I15–J-I17).
The largest design moves of the round: a building's stored wings are carried with its outline by one rule, so a dragged
street moves roofs and the 3-D export with the ground (§2.6, J-P42); a canal or a moved river that crosses a town wall
re-derives exactly the blocks it names, the wall's new strips included (§4.11, J-P34); S1's water operator clears its
whole footprint before the water goes in (§7.3 (b), J-DET-23); a saved town recomputes its own fingerprint before the
first edit (§2.9, J-DET-38, J-DET-40); every generated name remembers its naming inputs, so choosing a name list or
editing a naming rule renames exactly the names it made, after confirmation (§6.3, J-DET-37, J-DET-41); kernel levels
follow the rows, so every removal restores what its insertion changed (§2.2 rule 3, J-K28), water gates name their bank
vertices as bridges do (J-K29, J-I15) and part of a chain is addressed by a `StretchRef` (§3.4, J-K31); the arena's outer
ring is an L0 chain and the map border an L1 chain (§4.1, J-P46, J-I16); a river crossing becomes a bridge only where a
road uses it (§4.2 S1 (5), J-P44, J-I17); a walled port's harbour road leaves through a gate on the harbour side (§4.2 S3
(4), J-P45); the derivation pool is kept at each size where it measures faster (§12.3, J-R44); rasters are resumable and
exports wait for the view (§8.9, J-R40, J-R46, J-R47); pinned labels never overlap (§9.2, J-R45); every gating timing is
taken alone on the runner (§12.1, J-R49); the tree stamps ship with the product (§8.10, J-R42, J-R48); and the document
checks bind every seat that writes this document (§17.1 (2), J-S32). Every item ends FIXED — none INVALID, none staged,
none deferred; the per-item table is `docs/ARCHITECTURE-VALIDATION.md` §R4. All six seats were opus stand-ins for Fable
seats, so every section this round changed carries a banner naming `fixall/integrate-r4`.

#### former §0.11 Fix-all round 5 (2026-09-26): the items still open after round 4


Round 4's five verification seats (`docs/validation/fix-all/r4-verify-*.md`) accepted every round-4 item (43 of 43) and
listed 35 new defects the round-4 revision had introduced or exposed (1 major, 34 minor); the determinism area found one
more while fixing them (D5-1). The five areas drafted fixes (`docs/validation/fix-all/r5-*.md`: kernel 6 items,
determinism 11, pipeline 8, rendering 6, scope 5), and the round-5 integration seat merged them, applied every request
one area made of another's sections, and settled two disagreements the drafts could not see in one another (C-1 and C-2
of `docs/validation/fix-all/r5-integration-log.md`; new judgment calls J-I18 and J-I19). The largest design moves of the
round: a saved town's fingerprint is checked only by the program version that made it — an old town is shown labelled
"made with an older version; not verified by this one", and an upgrade checks a kept town under the old version and
records its new fingerprint under the new one (§2.9, §7.5, J-DET-44); "trust the saved town" and saving wait for the
fingerprint check (J-DET-45); a name freed by an undo or an unlock is re-drawn from a changed list only after
confirmation, and every confirmed rename is stored with its edit (§7.3 item 4, J-DET-46, J-DET-47); a street list chosen
for one culture renames only its streets (§7.6, J-DET-48); the records that steer faithful regeneration and naming have
their own fingerprint (§6.2, J-DET-49); a town kept from its saved copy is shared as its file, never as a link (§6.4,
J-DET-50); the search index never answers with an item's old level (§3.3, J-K34); water gates made with the town and by
an edit both stay put under the street-level warp (§3.6, J-K35); a stored manor-style composition moves with its house
(§2.6, J-P48), and the roof outlines follow every change of a house outline, in generation as in editing (J-P47); the
tree stamps meet without seams (§8.10, J-R50); a large print places its labels in small steps (§9.2, J-R51); tree
positions are the same on every machine (J-R52); a moving sun lights roofs and crowns in whole shared steps (§8.4,
J-R54); M1 builds the saved-file format it measures (§16, J-S40); the D-5 panel pairs PLAT with FTG's recorded
populations (§13.6, J-S41); and the effort estimate rises from 150–200 to **153–204 expert person-weeks**, because the
components rounds 4 and 5 added are now itemised and priced (§16, D-20, J-S43) — an owner-visible number, stated here.
Every item ends FIXED — none INVALID, none staged, none deferred; the one owner-staged subject touched (stage 3, NP4-2)
has its hook made concrete; the per-item table is `docs/ARCHITECTURE-VALIDATION.md` §R5. All six seats were opus
stand-ins for Fable seats, so every section this round changed carries a banner naming `fixall/integrate-r5`.
